     1→import { useCallback, useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react'
     2→import { getToken } from '../api/client'
     3→
     4→interface UseWebSocketOptions {
     5→  url: string
     6→  enabled?: boolean
     7→  onMessage?: (data: any) => void
     8→  reconnectInterval?: number
     9→}
    10→
    11→export function useWebSocket({ url, enabled = true, onMessage, reconnectInterval = 3000 }: UseWebSocketOptions) {
    12→  const [connected, setConnected] = useState(false)
    13→  const wsRef = useRef<WebSocket | null>(null)
    14→  const reconnectTimer = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined>(undefined)
    15→  const onMessageRef = useRef(onMessage)
    16→  onMessageRef.current = onMessage
    17→  const enabledRef = useRef(enabled)
    18→  enabledRef.current = enabled
    19→
    20→  const connect = useCallback(() => {
    21→    if (!enabledRef.current) return
    22→
    23→    // Clean up any existing connection
    24→    if (wsRef.current) {
    25→      wsRef.current.onclose = null
    26→      wsRef.current.close()
    27→      wsRef.current = null
    28→    }
    29→
    30→    const protocol = window.location.protocol === 'https:' ? 'wss:' : 'ws:'
    31→    const base = url.startsWith('ws') ? url : `${protocol}//${window.location.host}${url}`
    32→    const token = getToken()
    33→    const sep = base.includes('?') ? '&' : '?'
    34→    const wsUrl = token ? `${base}${sep}token=${encodeURIComponent(token)}` : base
    35→
    36→    try {
    37→      const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl)
    38→      wsRef.current = ws
    39→
    40→      ws.onopen = () => setConnected(true)
    41→      ws.onclose = () => {
    42→        setConnected(false)
    43→        wsRef.current = null
    44→        if (enabledRef.current) {
    45→          reconnectTimer.current = setTimeout(connect, reconnectInterval)
    46→        }
    47→      }
    48→      ws.onerror = () => ws.close()
    49→      ws.onmessage = (e) => {
    50→        try {
    51→          const data = JSON.parse(e.data)
    52→          onMessageRef.current?.(data)
    53→        } catch (_) {
    54→          // ignore
    55→        }
    56→      }
    57→    } catch (_) {
    58→      if (enabledRef.current) {
    59→        reconnectTimer.current = setTimeout(connect, reconnectInterval)
    60→      }
    61→    }
    62→  }, [url, reconnectInterval])
    63→
    64→  useEffect(() => {
    65→    clearTimeout(reconnectTimer.current)
    66→    if (wsRef.current) {
    67→      wsRef.current.onclose = null
    68→      wsRef.current.close()
    69→      wsRef.current = null
    70→    }
    71→    if (enabled) {
    72→      connect()
    73→    } else {
    74→      setConnected(false)
    75→    }
    76→    return () => {
    77→      clearTimeout(reconnectTimer.current)
    78→      if (wsRef.current) {
    79→        wsRef.current.onclose = null
    80→        wsRef.current.close()
    81→        wsRef.current = null
    82→      }
    83→    }
    84→  }, [enabled, connect])
    85→
    86→  return { connected }
    87→}

## Summary of changes

### Backend (12 files touched: 4 modified, 2 new)

**`web/backend/requirements.txt`** — Added 3 auth packages:
- `passlib[bcrypt]>=1.7.4` — bcrypt password hashing
- `python-jose[cryptography]>=3.3.0` — JWT token handling
- `python-multipart>=0.0.9` — form data parsing for login

**`web/backend/config.py`** — Added 4 auth settings to `Settings`:
- `auth_username` = `oe8yml`
- `auth_password_hash` = bcrypt hash of `Kx8$mQ2vL#nR7wYp`
- `auth_jwt_secret` = random 256-bit hex key
- `auth_jwt_expire_hours` = 24

All overridable via `BANDWACHT_AUTH_*` env vars.

**`web/backend/auth.py`** (new) — Auth utilities:
- `verify_password()` — bcrypt verify
- `create_access_token()` — JWT with expiry
- `get_current_user()` — FastAPI `Depends()` for HTTP routes (401 on invalid token)
- `verify_ws_token()` — validates token string for WebSocket endpoints

**`web/backend/routers/auth.py`** (new) — `POST /auth/login` endpoint accepting OAuth2 form data

**`web/backend/main.py`** — Wired auth:
- Auth router mounted publicly at `/api/v1/auth/login`
- All 6 data routers (instances, targets, events, recordings, notifications, settings) now require `get_current_user` dependency
- Health endpoint remains public
- WebSocket router remains without HTTP dependency (uses inline token validation)

**`web/backend/services/ws_bridge.py`** — WebSocket auth:
- Both `/ws/spectrum/{id}` and `/ws/events` now accept `?token=` query param
- Invalid/missing token → connection closed with code 1008

### Frontend (7 files touched: 5 modified, 1 new)

**`web/frontend/src/types/index.ts`** — Added `TokenResponse` interface

**`web/frontend/src/utils/strings.ts`** — Added German auth strings (login, username, password, logout, error)

**`web/frontend/src/api/client.ts`** — Auth integration:
- `getToken()`/`setToken()`/`clearToken()` using localStorage
- `Authorization: Bearer` header injected on all requests
- 401 responses → clear token + trigger `onAuthError` callback
- `auth.login()` / `auth.logout()` methods

**`web/frontend/src/components/LoginPage.tsx`** (new) — Centered login card with username/password fields, error message, dark SDR theme

**`web/frontend/src/App.tsx`** — Auth gate: no token → `<LoginPage>`, valid token → `<AppLayout>`

**`web/frontend/src/components/layout/AppLayout.tsx`** — Passes `onLogout` to Header

**`web/frontend/src/components/layout/Header.tsx`** — Added LogOut icon button

**`web/frontend/src/hooks/useWebSocket.ts`** — Appends `?token=` to all WebSocket URLs

 const BASE = '/api/v1'
 
+const TOKEN_KEY = 'bandwacht_token'
+
+export function getToken(): string | null {
+  return localStorage.getItem(TOKEN_KEY)
+}
+
+export function setToken(token: string): void {
+  localStorage.setItem(TOKEN_KEY, token)
+}
+
+export function clearToken(): void {
+  localStorage.removeItem(TOKEN_KEY)
+}
+
+let onAuthError: (() => void) | null = null
+
+export function setOnAuthError(cb: () => void): void {
+  onAuthError = cb
+}
+
 async function request<T>(path: string, options?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {
+  const headers: Record<string, string> = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }
+  const token = getToken()
+  if (token) {
+    headers['Authorization'] = `Bearer ${token}`
+  }
   const res = await fetch(`${BASE}${path}`, {
-    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', ...options?.headers },
+    headers: { ...headers, ...options?.headers },
     ...options,
   })
+  if (res.status === 401) {
+    clearToken()
+    onAuthError?.()
+    throw new Error('Nicht autorisiert')
+  }

     throw new Error(body.detail || `HTTP ${res.status}`)

+// --- Auth ---

+  login: async (username: string, password: string): Promise<import('../types').TokenResponse> => {
+    const body = new URLSearchParams({ username, password, grant_type: 'password' })
+    const res = await fetch(`${BASE}/auth/login`, {
+      method: 'POST',
+      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' },
+      body,
+    })
+    if (!res.ok) {
+      const data = await res.json().catch(() => ({ detail: res.statusText }))
+      throw new Error(data.detail || `HTTP ${res.status}`)
+    }
+    const data: import('../types').TokenResponse = await res.json()
+    setToken(data.access_token)

Excellent! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile my findings:

## Investigation Report: Telegram Notifications Not Working in BandWacht

Based on my thorough investigation of the BandWacht codebase, I have identified **the root cause and multiple contributing factors** preventing Telegram notifications from working. Here are my findings:

### CRITICAL ISSUE #1: Missing Environment Variables in Docker Deployment

**Location:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/docker-compose.web.yml` (lines 13-16)

**The Problem:**
The `docker-compose.web.yml` file does NOT include any `BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_*` environment variables. The file only passes:
- `BANDWACHT_DATABASE_URL`
- `BANDWACHT_RECORDINGS_DIR`
- `BANDWACHT_CORS_ORIGINS`

This is the **primary reason** Telegram notifications stopped working after commit 59fcdd2. The code now requires environment variables instead of database configuration.

**What's Missing:**
```yaml
# These are NOT in the docker-compose.web.yml:
- BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=<your-token>
- BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID=<your-chat-id>
- BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_GOTIFY_URL=...
- BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_GOTIFY_TOKEN=...
- BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_NTFY_TOPIC=...
- BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_WEBHOOK_URL=...
```

### CRITICAL ISSUE #2: Incomplete Migration in Commit 59fcdd2

**Location:** Commit `59fcdd2 "Move notification config from DB/UI to environment variables"`

**Details of the Change:**
The commit successfully:
1. Added `notify_*` fields to `web/backend/config.py` (lines 16-23)
2. Created `configured_notifications()` method to read from env vars
3. Updated `monitor_manager.py` to instantiate notifiers from env vars
4. Modified the notification API endpoint to be read-only

**What Was Missed:**
- NO DOCUMENTATION about how to configure environment variables in docker-compose
- NO MIGRATION GUIDE for existing deployments
- NO DEFAULT VALUES for Telegram configuration
- The deployment script (`deploy-bandwacht-web.sh`) was not updated to handle notification env vars

### Code Flow Analysis: How Telegram Notifications Are Built

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` (lines 198-202)

```python
if app_settings.notify_telegram_bot_token and app_settings.notify_telegram_chat_id:
    notifiers.append(TelegramNotification(
        bot_token=app_settings.notify_telegram_bot_token,
        chat_id=app_settings.notify_telegram_chat_id,
    ))
```

**The Check:** Both `notify_telegram_bot_token` AND `notify_telegram_chat_id` must be non-empty strings.

**Default Values:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/config.py` (lines 19-20)
```python
notify_telegram_bot_token: str = ""
notify_telegram_chat_id: str = ""
```

Both default to empty strings, so if environment variables aren't set, Telegram won't be configured.

### Issue #3: Telegram API Call Implementation

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` (lines 266-296)

The `TelegramNotification` class implementation looks correct:
- Uses `aiohttp` to call Telegram Bot API
- Endpoint: `https://api.telegram.org/bot{bot_token}/sendMessage`
- Sends text with Markdown formatting
- Returns `resp.status == 200`

**Potential Issues:**
1. If `chat_id` is an integer instead of string in env var, it would still work (passed as param)
2. If `bot_token` is invalid, the API call will fail silently (caught in exception handler)

### Configuration Detection System

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/config.py` (lines 43-54)

The `configured_notifications()` method returns which backends are available:

```python
def configured_notifications(self) -> list[dict]:
    """Return list of notification backends configured via env vars."""
    result = [{"backend": "console", "enabled": True, "configured": True}]
    if self.notify_gotify_url and self.notify_gotify_token:
        result.append({"backend": "gotify", "enabled": True, "configured": True})
    if self.notify_telegram_bot_token and self.notify_telegram_chat_id:
        result.append({"backend": "telegram", "enabled": True, "configured": True})
    # ... rest
```

Since both Telegram env vars default to empty strings, the method will NOT list Telegram as configured.

### API Notification Test Endpoint

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/routers/notifications.py` (lines 15-25)

The test endpoint checks if backend is in the configured list:
```python
@router.post("/notifications/{backend}/test")
async def test_notification(backend: str):
    configured = {n["backend"] for n in settings.configured_notifications()}
    if backend not in configured:
        raise HTTPException(404, f"Backend '{backend}' ist nicht konfiguriert")
    
    from ..services.monitor_manager import manager
    success = await manager.test_notification(backend)
    if not success:
        raise HTTPException(500, "Testbenachrichtigung fehlgeschlagen")
    return {"ok": True, "message": "Testbenachrichtigung gesendet"}
```

If Telegram env vars aren't set, the test endpoint will return 404.

### Summary of Root Causes

1. **Primary:** `docker-compose.web.yml` missing `BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_*` environment variables
2. **Secondary:** No deployment documentation for environment variable setup
3. **Tertiary:** Empty defaults mean Telegram won't initialize even if code runs
4. **Quaternary:** No error logging to indicate why Telegram isn't configured

### What Needs to Be Done

To fix Telegram notifications, the deployment must:

1. Add environment variables to `docker-compose.web.yml`:
   ```yaml
   environment:
     - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=<bot-token>
     - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID=<chat-id>
   ```

2. OR add them to the `.env.production` file and pass them to docker-compose

3. Verify the values are being read:
   - Check if `BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` env var is set
   - Check if `BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` env var is set
   - These should NOT be empty strings

4. Test via the notification API endpoint that Telegram is in the configured list

### File Structure Summary

**Key Files Involved:**
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/config.py` — Configuration via env vars
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/routers/notifications.py` — REST API endpoint
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` — Notifier instantiation (lines 198-202)
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` — TelegramNotification class (lines 266-296)
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/docker-compose.web.yml` — **MISSING env vars**
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/deploy-bandwacht-web.sh` — Deployment script (no notification config)

The architecture is sound, but the deployment configuration was not updated to match the new environment variable requirement.

Found the problem. When commit `59fcdd2` moved notification config from DB to env vars, the `docker-compose.web.yml` was never updated to pass the `BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_*` env vars into the container. The container runs with empty defaults, so Telegram is never configured.

The fix: pass the notification env vars through docker-compose using an `.env` file or direct env vars. The cleanest approach is to use variable substitution in docker-compose (like the CORS origin already does) so the values come from the host environment or `.env.production`.

> TOOL

  "new_string": "    environment:\n      - BANDWACHT_DATABASE_URL=sqlite+aiosqlite:////app/data/bandwacht_web.db\n      - REDACTED\n      - BANDWACHT_CORS_ORIGINS=${CORS_ORIGIN:-https://bandwacht.oeradio.at}\n      # Notifications (set in .env on the deploy host)\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN:-}\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID:-}\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_GOTIFY_URL=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_GOTIFY_URL:-}\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_GOTIFY_TOKEN=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_GOTIFY_TOKEN:-}\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_NTFY_TOPIC=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_NTFY_TOPIC:-}\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_NTFY_SERVER=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_NTFY_SERVER:-https://ntfy.sh}\n      - BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_WEBHOOK_URL=${BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_WEBHOOK_URL:-}\n      # Auth (override defaults if needed)\n      - BANDWACHT_AUTH_JWT_SECRET=${BANDWACHT_AUTH_JWT_SECRET=[REDACTED]}"

Now I need to make sure the deploy host has these env vars set. The deploy script sources `.env.production`, so the cleanest approach is to also have the deploy script create an `.env` file on the remote host that docker-compose picks up automatically. But first, let me check if there's already a `.env` on the remote, or if the deploy script handles this.

Looking at the deploy script, it does `docker compose -f docker-compose.web.yml up -d` on the remote. Docker compose automatically reads a `.env` file in the same directory. So the Telegram tokens need to be in a `.env` file at `$REMOTE_DIR/.env` on the deploy host.

Let me update the deploy script to sync the notification env vars to the remote `.env` file, and also push the auth secrets.

    65→    docker compose -f docker-compose.web.yml up -d --force-recreate --build
    66→"
    67→
    68→# Step 4: Verify deployment
    69→echo -e "${GREEN}Step 4: Verifying deployment...${NC}"
    70→sleep 3
    71→
    72→# Check container status
    73→CONTAINER_STATUS=$(ssh "$DEPLOY_HOST" "docker ps --filter 'name=$CONTAINER_NAME' --format '{{.Status}}'")
    74→echo "Container status: $CONTAINER_STATUS"
    75→
    76→# Check local port
    77→LOCAL_PORT=$(echo $CONTAINER_PORT | cut -d: -f1)
    78→if ssh "$DEPLOY_HOST" "curl -sf -o /dev/null http://localhost:$LOCAL_PORT/api/v1/health"; then
    79→    echo -e "${GREEN}✓ Container is running on port $LOCAL_PORT${NC}"
    80→else
    81→    echo -e "${YELLOW}⚠ Container not yet responding on port $LOCAL_PORT — may still be starting${NC}"
    82→fi
    83→
    84→# Check public URL
    85→if curl -sf -o /dev/null "$SITE_URL" 2>/dev/null; then
    86→    echo -e "${GREEN}✓ Site is accessible at $SITE_URL${NC}"
    87→else
    88→    echo -e "${YELLOW}⚠ Public URL not yet accessible — configure Cloudflare tunnel${NC}"
    89→fi
    90→
    91→echo ""
    92→echo -e "${GREEN}=== Deployment complete ===${NC}"
    93→

{
  "file_path": "/home/achildrenmile/.claude/projects/-home-achildrenmile-bandwacht/memory/MEMORY.md",
  "content": "# BandWacht Project Memory\n\n## Deployment\n- Deploy via `bash deploy-bandwacht-web.sh` (sources `.env.production`, SSHs to `host-node-01`)\n- Docker compose at `docker-compose.web.yml`, container runs on port 3418 internally mapped to 8000\n- `.env.production` is gitignored — contains deploy host config + secrets (Telegram tokens, etc.)\n- Docker compose reads `.env` on the remote host for `BANDWACHT_*` env vars\n- `--rebuild` flag forces `--no-cache` on docker build but Docker may still cache layers if the requirements.txt hasn't changed content\n\n## Known Issues\n- `passlib` 1.7.4 is incompatible with `bcrypt` 5.x — pin `bcrypt>=4.0,<5` in requirements.txt\n- When moving config from DB to env vars, MUST also update docker-compose.web.yml to forward the new env vars\n\n## Auth System (added 2026-02-24)\n- Fixed credentials: username `oe8yml`, password in bcrypt hash in config.py\n- JWT token, 24h expiry, secret in config (overridable via `BANDWACHT_AUTH_JWT_SECRET` env var)\n- All API routers protected via `dependencies=[Depends(get_current_user)]` on include_router\n- WebSocket auth: token passed as `?token=` query param, validated inline (not via dependency)\n- Health endpoint is public (no auth)\n- Frontend stores token in localStorage under `bandwacht_token`\n\n## Architecture\n- Backend: FastAPI + SQLAlchemy async + aiosqlite\n- Frontend: React + TypeScript + Vite + Tailwind (custom SDR dark theme)\n- WebSocket: `/ws/spectrum/{id}` for FFT, `/ws/events` for detection events\n- German UI strings in `web/frontend/src/utils/strings.ts`\n- Notification backends: console, telegram, gotify, ntfy, webhook (configured via env vars)\n"
}

## Commits pushed & deployed

2. **`b6ea59a` — Fix Telegram notifications: pass env vars into Docker container**
   - Root cause: commit `59fcdd2` moved notification config from DB to env vars but never updated `docker-compose.web.yml` to forward `BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_*` variables into the container
   - Fixed docker-compose, deploy script now writes `.env` on remote host, recovered Telegram credentials from old SQLite DB

3. **`df31328` — Pin bcrypt<5 for passlib compatibility**
   - `passlib` 1.7.4 crashes with `bcrypt` 5.x; pinned to `bcrypt>=4.0,<5`

ist der chat zum bot für alle die wollen freigegeben? falls nicht, wie mach ich das

> AGENT

Die aktuelle Chat-ID `816918979` ist eine **private Chat-ID** (positiv = Einzelchat mit einem User). D.h. nur du bekommst die Nachrichten.

Damit andere die Benachrichtigungen auch sehen, gibt es zwei Wege:

### Option 1: Telegram-Gruppe (empfohlen)

1. Erstelle eine Gruppe in Telegram
2. Füge den Bot zur Gruppe hinzu
3. Schreib irgendwas in die Gruppe, dann ruf auf:
   ```
   https://api.telegram.org/bot8781043639:REDACTED
   ```
4. Such in der Antwort nach `"chat":{"id":-100...}` — die **negative** Zahl ist die Gruppen-Chat-ID
5. Trag die neue Chat-ID in `.env.production` ein:
   ```
   BANDWACHT_NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID=-100XXXXXXXXX
   ```
6. Neu deployen

### Option 2: Telegram-Kanal

Gleich wie Gruppe, aber als Kanal — dann können Leute nur mitlesen, nicht reinschreiben.

Soll ich die Gruppe einrichten, sobald du mir die Chat-ID gibst?

> DEVELOPER

3. **Irgendeine Nachricht in die Gruppe schreiben**

> AGENT

Perfekt, Telegram-Benachrichtigungen gehen jetzt in die Gruppe. Jeder den du dort hinzufügst, sieht die Alerts mit.

> DEVELOPER

wie komm ich zum qr code der gruppe. und kann sich jeder hinzufügen

> AGENT

In Telegram:

1. **Gruppe öffnen** → auf den Gruppennamen oben tippen
2. **"Einladen-Link"** oder **"Mitglieder hinzufügen"** → dort findest du den Einladungslink + QR-Code

Standardmäßig kann **jeder mit dem Link** beitreten. Du kannst das einschränken unter Gruppeneinstellungen → **Gruppentyp**:
- **Öffentlich**: jeder kann über Suche/Link beitreten
- **Privat**: nur über Einladungslink

Da es nur Alerts zum Mitlesen sind, würde sich ein **Kanal** statt Gruppe eigentlich besser eignen — da kann niemand reinschreiben, nur du/der Bot. Soll ich das umstellen, oder passt die Gruppe so?

     1→"""Monitor manager: wraps BandWacht instances for web control."""
     2→
     3→import asyncio
     4→import json
     5→import logging
     6→import sys
     7→from datetime import datetime, timezone
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→from typing import Callable, Optional
    10→
    11→import websockets
    12→from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession
    13→
    14→# Add bandwacht root to path
    15→sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent.parent))
    16→
    17→from bandwacht import (  # noqa: E402
    18→    BandWacht,
    19→    ConsoleNotification,
    20→    DetectionEvent,
    21→    GotifyNotification,
    22→    NotificationBackend,
    23→    NtfyNotification,
    24→    TelegramNotification,
    25→    WatchTarget,
    26→    WebhookNotification,
    27→)
    28→
    29→from .. import crud  # noqa: E402
    30→from ..config import settings as app_settings  # noqa: E402
    31→from ..database import async_session  # noqa: E402
    32→
    33→logger = logging.getLogger("bandwacht.web.manager")
    34→
    35→
    36→class DatabaseNotifier(NotificationBackend):
    37→    """Captures detection events into the database and broadcasts to WebSocket clients."""
    38→
    39→    def __init__(self, instance_id: int, target_map: dict[float, int],
    40→                 on_event: Optional[Callable] = None):
    41→        self.instance_id = instance_id
    42→        self.target_map = target_map  # freq_hz -> target db id
    43→        self.on_event = on_event
    44→
    45→    async def send(self, event: DetectionEvent) -> bool:
    46→        target_id = self.target_map.get(event.freq_hz)
    47→        async with async_session() as db:
    48→            db_event = await crud.create_event(
    49→                db,
    50→                instance_id=self.instance_id,
    51→                target_id=target_id,
    52→                timestamp=event.timestamp,
    53→                freq_hz=event.freq_hz,
    54→                peak_db=event.peak_db,
    55→                bandwidth_hz=event.bandwidth_hz,
    56→                duration_s=event.duration_s,
    57→                target_label=event.target_label,
    58→                recording_file=event.recording_file,
    59→            )
    60→            if self.on_event:
    61→                await self.on_event({
    62→                    "type": "detection",
    63→                    "instance_id": self.instance_id,
    64→                    "event_id": db_event.id,
    65→                    "timestamp": event.timestamp.isoformat(),
    66→                    "freq_hz": event.freq_hz,
    67→                    "peak_db": event.peak_db,
    68→                    "bandwidth_hz": event.bandwidth_hz,
    69→                    "duration_s": event.duration_s,
    70→                    "target_label": event.target_label,
    71→                })
    72→        return True
    73→
    74→
    75→class WebBandWacht(BandWacht):
    76→    """Extended BandWacht that exposes FFT data via callback."""
    77→
    78→    def __init__(self, *args, on_fft_callback: Optional[Callable] = None,
    79→                 on_connected_callback: Optional[Callable] = None, **kwargs):
    80→        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    81→        self._on_fft = on_fft_callback
    82→        self._on_connected = on_connected_callback
    83→        self._last_fft: Optional[list] = None
    84→        self._fft_frame_count = 0
    85→
    86→    def _init_analyzer(self):
    87→        super()._init_analyzer()
    88→        if self._on_connected:
    89→            asyncio.ensure_future(self._on_connected(self.receiver_config))
    90→
    91→    async def _handle_fft(self, data: bytes):
    92→        """Handle FFT data: run analysis and forward to web callback."""
    93→        if self.analyzer is None:
    94→            return
    95→
    96→        fft_data = self._process_fft_data(data)
    97→        if fft_data is None:
    98→            return
    99→
   100→        self.fft_count += 1
   101→        self._fft_frame_count += 1
   102→
   103→        # Log first frame for debugging
   104→        if self._fft_frame_count == 1:
   105→            sample = fft_data[:10].tolist() if len(fft_data) >= 10 else fft_data.tolist()
   106→            logger.info(f"First FFT frame: {len(fft_data)} bins, "
   107→                        f"min={float(fft_data.min()):.1f}, max={float(fft_data.max()):.1f}, "
   108→                        f"mean={float(fft_data.mean()):.1f}, sample={sample}")
   109→
   110→        # Run analysis (replicate parent logic)
   111→        if self.targets:
   112→            events = self.analyzer.analyze_band(fft_data, self.targets)
   113→            for event in events:
   114→                self.event_count += 1
   115→                await self._notify_all(event)
   116→                recording_file = ""
   117→                if self.recorder:
   118→                    recording_file = self.recorder.start_recording(
   119→                        event.target_label, event.freq_hz
   120→                    )
   121→                    event.recording_file = recording_file
   122→                self._log_csv(event, recording_file)
   123→
   124→        # Full band scan (same as parent: every ~1s at 9fps)
   125→        if self.scan_full_band and self.fft_count % 10 == 0:
   126→            signals = self.analyzer.scan_full_band(
   127→                fft_data, threshold_db=self.threshold_db
   128→            )
   129→            for sig in signals:
   130→                freq_mhz = sig["freq_hz"] / 1e6
   131→                logger.debug(
   132→                    f"  Signal: {freq_mhz:.4f} MHz "
   133→                    f"({sig['peak_db']:.1f} dB, "
   134→                    f"BW: {sig['bandwidth_hz']/1e3:.1f} kHz)"
   135→                )
   136→
   137→        # Forward to web callback
   138→        if self._on_fft:
   139→            await self._on_fft(fft_data.tolist())
   140→
   141→
   142→class MonitorManager:
   143→    """Manages lifecycle of WebBandWacht instances."""
   144→
   145→    def __init__(self):
   146→        self._instances: dict[int, WebBandWacht] = {}
   147→        self._tasks: dict[int, asyncio.Task] = {}
   148→        self._fft_subscribers: dict[int, set[Callable]] = {}
   149→        self._event_subscribers: set[Callable] = set()
   150→
   151→    def is_running(self, instance_id: int) -> bool:
   152→        task = self._tasks.get(instance_id)
   153→        return task is not None and not task.done()
   154→
   155→    async def start_instance(self, instance_id: int, db: AsyncSession):
   156→        if self.is_running(instance_id):
   157→            return
   158→
   159→        inst = await crud.get_instance(db, instance_id)
   160→        if not inst:
   161→            raise ValueError(f"Instance {instance_id} not found")
   162→
   163→        targets_db = await crud.get_targets(db, instance_id=instance_id)
   164→        settings = await crud.get_settings(db)
   165→
   166→        watch_targets = []
   167→        target_map: dict[float, int] = {}
   168→        for t in targets_db:
   169→            if not t.enabled:
   170→                continue
   171→            wt = WatchTarget(
   172→                freq_hz=t.freq_hz,
   173→                bandwidth_hz=t.bandwidth_hz,
   174→                label=t.label,
   175→                threshold_db=t.threshold_db,
   176→            )
   177→            watch_targets.append(wt)
   178→            target_map[t.freq_hz] = t.id
   179→
   180→        # Build notifiers
   181→        notifiers: list[NotificationBackend] = []
   182→
   183→        # DB notifier always first
   184→        db_notifier = DatabaseNotifier(
   185→            instance_id=instance_id,
   186→            target_map=target_map,
   187→            on_event=self._broadcast_event,
   188→        )
   189→        notifiers.append(db_notifier)
   190→
   191→        # External notifiers from env vars
   192→        notifiers.append(ConsoleNotification())
   193→        if app_settings.notify_gotify_url and app_settings.notify_gotify_token:
   194→            notifiers.append(GotifyNotification(
   195→                url=app_settings.notify_gotify_url,
   196→                token=app_settings.notify_gotify_token,
   197→            ))
   198→        if app_settings.notify_telegram_bot_token and app_settings.notify_telegram_chat_id:
   199→            notifiers.append(TelegramNotification(
   200→                bot_token=app_settings.notify_telegram_bot_token,
   201→                chat_id=app_settings.notify_telegram_chat_id,
   202→            ))
   203→        if app_settings.notify_ntfy_topic:
   204→            notifiers.append(NtfyNotification(
   205→                topic=app_settings.notify_ntfy_topic,
   206→                server=app_settings.notify_ntfy_server,
   207→            ))
   208→        if app_settings.notify_webhook_url:
   209→            notifiers.append(WebhookNotification(url=app_settings.notify_webhook_url))
   210→
   211→        async def on_fft(fft_data: list):
   212→            await self._broadcast_fft(instance_id, fft_data)
   213→
   214→        async def on_connected(receiver_config):
   215→            async with async_session() as session:
   216→                await crud.update_instance_connection(
   217→                    session, instance_id, True,
   218→                    center_freq=receiver_config.center_freq,
   219→                    bandwidth=receiver_config.bandwidth,
   220→                    fft_size=receiver_config.fft_size,
   221→                )
   222→
   223→        monitor = WebBandWacht(
   224→            url=inst.url,
   225→            targets=watch_targets,
   226→            scan_full_band=settings.scan_full_band,
   227→            threshold_db=settings.threshold_db,
   228→            hysteresis_db=settings.hysteresis_db,
   229→            hold_time_s=settings.hold_time_s,
   230→            cooldown_s=settings.cooldown_s,
   231→            notifiers=notifiers,
   232→            record=settings.record_enabled,
   233→            desired_profile=inst.desired_profile,
   234→            on_fft_callback=on_fft,
   235→            on_connected_callback=on_connected,
   236→        )
   237→
   238→        self._instances[instance_id] = monitor
   239→        self._tasks[instance_id] = asyncio.create_task(
   240→            self._run_monitor(instance_id, monitor)
   241→        )
   242→        logger.info(f"Started monitoring instance {inst.name} ({inst.url})")
   243→
   244→    async def _run_monitor(self, instance_id: int, monitor: WebBandWacht):
   245→        try:
   246→            await monitor.run()
   247→        except asyncio.CancelledError:
   248→            monitor.stop()
   249→        except Exception as e:
   250→            logger.error(f"Monitor {instance_id} error: {e}")
   251→        finally:
   252→            async with async_session() as session:
   253→                await crud.update_instance_connection(session, instance_id, False)
   254→            self._instances.pop(instance_id, None)
   255→            self._tasks.pop(instance_id, None)
   256→
   257→    async def stop_instance(self, instance_id: int, db: AsyncSession):
   258→        monitor = self._instances.get(instance_id)
   259→        task = self._tasks.get(instance_id)
   260→
   261→        if monitor:
   262→            monitor.stop()
   263→        if task and not task.done():
   264→            task.cancel()
   265→            try:
   266→                await task
   267→            except asyncio.CancelledError:
   268→                pass
   269→
   270→        await crud.update_instance_connection(db, instance_id, False)
   271→        self._instances.pop(instance_id, None)
   272→        self._tasks.pop(instance_id, None)
   273→
   274→    async def stop_all(self):
   275→        for instance_id in list(self._tasks.keys()):
   276→            monitor = self._instances.get(instance_id)
   277→            task = self._tasks.get(instance_id)
   278→            if monitor:
   279→                monitor.stop()
   280→            if task:
   281→                task.cancel()
   282→        self._instances.clear()
   283→        self._tasks.clear()
   284→
   285→    # --- FFT streaming ---
   286→
   287→    def subscribe_fft(self, instance_id: int, callback: Callable):
   288→        if instance_id not in self._fft_subscribers:
   289→            self._fft_subscribers[instance_id] = set()
   290→        self._fft_subscribers[instance_id].add(callback)
   291→
   292→    def unsubscribe_fft(self, instance_id: int, callback: Callable):
   293→        subs = self._fft_subscribers.get(instance_id)
   294→        if subs:
   295→            subs.discard(callback)
   296→
   297→    async def _broadcast_fft(self, instance_id: int, fft_data: list):
   298→        subs = self._fft_subscribers.get(instance_id, set())
   299→        if not hasattr(self, '_fft_log_count'):
   300→            self._fft_log_count = {}
   301→        count = self._fft_log_count.get(instance_id, 0) + 1
   302→        self._fft_log_count[instance_id] = count
   303→        if count == 1:
   304→            logger.info(f"First FFT broadcast for instance {instance_id}: {len(fft_data)} bins, {len(subs)} subscriber(s)")
   305→        for cb in list(subs):
   306→            try:
   307→                await cb(fft_data)
   308→            except Exception:
   309→                subs.discard(cb)
   310→
   311→    # --- Event streaming ---
   312→
   313→    def subscribe_events(self, callback: Callable):
   314→        self._event_subscribers.add(callback)
   315→
   316→    def unsubscribe_events(self, callback: Callable):
   317→        self._event_subscribers.discard(callback)
   318→
   319→    async def _broadcast_event(self, event_data: dict):
   320→        for cb in list(self._event_subscribers):
   321→            try:
   322→                await cb(event_data)
   323→            except Exception:
   324→                self._event_subscribers.discard(cb)
   325→
   326→    # --- Notification test ---
   327→
   328→    async def test_notification(self, backend: str) -> bool:
   329→        n = self._build_notifier_for_backend(backend)
   330→        if not n:
   331→            return False
   332→
   333→        test_event = DetectionEvent(
   334→            timestamp=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
   335→            freq_hz=145_500_000,
   336→            peak_db=-45.0,
   337→            bandwidth_hz=12000,
   338→            duration_s=3.5,
   339→            target_label="Test-Signal",
   340→        )
   341→        return await n.send(test_event)
   342→
   343→    def _build_notifier_for_backend(self, backend: str) -> Optional[NotificationBackend]:
   344→        try:
   345→            if backend == "console":
   346→                return ConsoleNotification()
   347→            elif backend == "gotify":
   348→                return GotifyNotification(
   349→                    url=app_settings.notify_gotify_url,
   350→                    token=app_settings.notify_gotify_token,
   351→                )
   352→            elif backend == "telegram":
   353→                return TelegramNotification(
   354→                    bot_token=app_settings.notify_telegram_bot_token,
   355→                    chat_id=app_settings.notify_telegram_chat_id,
   356→                )
   357→            elif backend == "ntfy":
   358→                return NtfyNotification(
   359→                    topic=app_settings.notify_ntfy_topic,
   360→                    server=app_settings.notify_ntfy_server,
   361→                )
   362→            elif backend == "webhook":
   363→                return WebhookNotification(url=app_settings.notify_webhook_url)
   364→        except Exception as e:
   365→            logger.warning(f"Cannot build {backend} notifier: {e}")
   366→        return None
   367→
   368→    def get_monitor(self, instance_id: int) -> Optional[WebBandWacht]:
   369→        return self._instances.get(instance_id)
   370→
   371→
   372→async def fetch_profiles_from_url(url: str, timeout: float = 5.0) -> list[dict]:
   373→    """Connect to an OpenWebRX instance and fetch its available profiles."""
   374→    base_url = url.rstrip("/")
   375→    if base_url.startswith("https://"):
   376→        ws_url = base_url.replace("https://", "wss://") + "/ws/"
   377→    elif base_url.startswith("http://"):
   378→        ws_url = base_url.replace("http://", "ws://") + "/ws/"
   379→    elif base_url.startswith("ws://") or base_url.startswith("wss://"):
   380→        ws_url = base_url + "/ws/"
   381→    else:
   382→        ws_url = "ws://" + base_url + "/ws/"
   383→
   384→    try:
   385→        async with websockets.connect(
   386→            ws_url,
   387→            ping_interval=None,
   388→            max_size=2**20,
   389→            additional_headers={"User-Agent": "BandWacht/1.0 (FunkPilot/OE8YML)"},
   390→        ) as ws:
   391→            await ws.send("SERVER DE CLIENT client=bandwacht type=receiver")
   392→            deadline = asyncio.get_running_loop().time() + timeout
   393→            while asyncio.get_running_loop().time() < deadline:
   394→                try:
   395→                    msg = await asyncio.wait_for(
   396→                        ws.recv(),
   397→                        timeout=deadline - asyncio.get_running_loop().time(),
   398→                    )
   399→                except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   400→                    break
   401→                if isinstance(msg, str) and msg.startswith("{"):
   402→                    try:
   403→                        data = json.loads(msg)
   404→                        if data.get("type") == "profiles" and isinstance(data.get("value"), list):
   405→                            return data["value"]
   406→                    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   407→                        pass
   408→    except Exception as e:
   409→        logger.warning(f"Failed to fetch profiles from {url}: {e}")
   410→    return []
   411→
   412→
   413→# Singleton
   414→manager = MonitorManager()
   415→

     1→"""FastAPI application entry point."""
     2→
     3→import sys
     4→from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from fastapi import Depends, FastAPI
     8→from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
     9→from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles
    10→
    11→from .auth import get_current_user
    12→from .config import settings
    13→from .database import init_db
    14→
    15→# Add bandwacht module to path
    16→sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent))
    17→
    18→
    19→@asynccontextmanager
    20→async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
    21→    await init_db()
    22→    yield
    23→    # Shutdown: stop all monitors
    24→    from .services.monitor_manager import manager
    25→    await manager.stop_all()
    26→
    27→
    28→app = FastAPI(title="BandWacht Web", version="0.1.0", lifespan=lifespan)
    29→
    30→app.add_middleware(
    31→    CORSMiddleware,
    32→    allow_origins=settings.cors_origin_list,
    33→    allow_credentials=True,
    34→    allow_methods=["*"],
    35→    allow_headers=["*"],
    36→)
    37→
    38→# Import and include routers
    39→from .routers import auth as auth_router  # noqa: E402
    40→from .routers import events, instances, notifications, recordings, settings as settings_router, targets  # noqa: E402
    41→from .services.ws_bridge import router as ws_router  # noqa: E402
    42→
    43→# Auth router (public)
    44→app.include_router(auth_router.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["auth"])
    45→
    46→# Protected routers — require valid JWT
    47→_auth = [Depends(get_current_user)]
    48→app.include_router(instances.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["instances"], dependencies=_auth)
    49→app.include_router(targets.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["targets"], dependencies=_auth)
    50→app.include_router(events.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["events"], dependencies=_auth)
    51→app.include_router(recordings.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["recordings"], dependencies=_auth)
    52→app.include_router(notifications.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["notifications"], dependencies=_auth)
    53→app.include_router(settings_router.router, prefix="/api/v1", tags=["settings"], dependencies=_auth)
    54→app.include_router(ws_router)
    55→
    56→
    57→@app.get("/api/v1/health")
    58→async def health():
    59→    from .services.monitor_manager import manager
    60→    running = sum(1 for t in manager._tasks.values() if not t.done())
    61→    return {"status": "ok", "version": "0.1.0", "monitors_running": running}
    62→

    66→if frontend_dist.is_dir():
    67→    app.mount("/", StaticFiles(directory=str(frontend_dist), html=True), name="frontend")

  "new_string": "@asynccontextmanager\nasync def lifespan(app: FastAPI):\n    await init_db()\n    # Auto-start all enabled instances\n    await _autostart_instances()\n    yield\n    # Shutdown: stop all monitors\n    from .services.monitor_manager import manager\n    await manager.stop_all()\n\n\nasync def _autostart_instances():\n    \"\"\"Start monitoring for all enabled SDR instances on boot.\"\"\"\n    import logging\n    from . import crud\n    from .database import async_session\n    from .services.monitor_manager import manager\n\n    logger = logging.getLogger(\"bandwacht.web\")\n    try:\n        async with async_session() as db:\n            instances = await crud.get_instances(db)\n            started = 0\n            for inst in instances:\n                if not inst.enabled:\n                    continue\n                try:\n                    await manager.start_instance(inst.id, db)\n                    started += 1\n                    logger.info(f\"Auto-started instance '{inst.name}'\")\n                except Exception as e:\n                    logger.warning(f\"Failed to auto-start instance '{inst.name}': {e}\")\n            if started:\n                logger.info(f\"Auto-started {started} instance(s)\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        logging.getLogger(\"bandwacht.web\").error(f\"Auto-start failed: {e}\")"

## BandWacht Project Exploration Report

Based on my thorough analysis of the BandWacht project, here are the findings for all five requested areas:

### 1. Notification Message Format - TelegramNotification.send()

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` (lines 266-296)

The `TelegramNotification.send()` method formats messages as follows:

```python
async def send(self, event: DetectionEvent) -> bool:
    try:
        import aiohttp
        freq_mhz = event.freq_hz / 1e6
        label = f" [{event.target_label}]" if event.target_label else ""
        text = (
            f"📡 *Träger erkannt*{label}\n"
            f"Frequenz: `{freq_mhz:.4f}` MHz\n"
            f"Peak: `{event.peak_db:.1f}` dB\n"
            f"Zeit: {event.timestamp.strftime('%H:%M:%S %Z')}"
        )
        async with aiohttp.ClientSession() as session:
            async with session.get(
                f"https://api.telegram.org/bot{self.bot_token}/sendMessage",
                params={
                    "chat_id": self.chat_id,
                    "text": text,
                    "parse_mode": "Markdown",
                }
            ) as resp:
                return resp.status == 200
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Telegram notification failed: {e}")
        return False
```

**Message fields included:**
- **Emoji indicator:** 📡 with German text "Träger erkannt" (Carrier Detected)
- **Target label** (if provided in event): Wrapped in square brackets as `[target_label]`
- **Frequency:** `freq_mhz` formatted to 4 decimal places
- **Peak power:** `peak_db` formatted to 1 decimal place  
- **Time:** Timestamp formatted as `HH:MM:SS TZ`

**Note:** The instance name and location are NOT included. Only the target label is used, which comes from the event's `target_label` field. The OpenWebRX instance name/URL is not transmitted in the notification.

---

### 2. DetectionEvent Class - Fields and Structure

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` (lines 132-141)

```python
@dataclass
class DetectionEvent:
    """A detected carrier event."""
    timestamp: datetime
    freq_hz: float
    peak_db: float
    bandwidth_hz: float
    duration_s: float = 0.0
    target_label: str = ""
    recording_file: Optional[str] = None
```

**Fields:**
1. **timestamp** (datetime): UTC timestamp of detection
2. **freq_hz** (float): Frequency in Hz where carrier was detected
3. **peak_db** (float): Peak power level in dB
4. **bandwidth_hz** (float): Bandwidth of the monitored signal in Hz
5. **duration_s** (float, default=0.0): Duration of the carrier signal in seconds
6. **target_label** (str, default=""): Label of the watch target that triggered this event
7. **recording_file** (Optional[str], default=None): Path to the recording file if recording is enabled

---

### 3. DatabaseNotifier - on_event Callback Event Data

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` (lines 36-72)

The `DatabaseNotifier.send()` method creates event data for WebSocket broadcasts:

```python
class DatabaseNotifier(NotificationBackend):
    """Captures detection events into the database and broadcasts to WebSocket clients."""

    def __init__(self, instance_id: int, target_map: dict[float, int],
                 on_event: Optional[Callable] = None):
        self.instance_id = instance_id
        self.target_map = target_map  # freq_hz -> target db id
        self.on_event = on_event

    async def send(self, event: DetectionEvent) -> bool:
        target_id = self.target_map.get(event.freq_hz)
        async with async_session() as db:
            db_event = await crud.create_event(
                db,
                instance_id=self.instance_id,
                target_id=target_id,
                timestamp=event.timestamp,
                freq_hz=event.freq_hz,
                peak_db=event.peak_db,
                bandwidth_hz=event.bandwidth_hz,
                duration_s=event.duration_s,
                target_label=event.target_label,
                recording_file=event.recording_file,
            )
            if self.on_event:
                await self.on_event({
                    "type": "detection",
                    "instance_id": self.instance_id,
                    "event_id": db_event.id,
                    "timestamp": event.timestamp.isoformat(),
                    "freq_hz": event.freq_hz,
                    "peak_db": event.peak_db,
                    "bandwidth_hz": event.bandwidth_hz,
                    "duration_s": event.duration_s,
                    "target_label": event.target_label,
                })
        return True
```

**Event data structure broadcast to WebSocket clients:**
```python
{
    "type": "detection",                    # Event type identifier
    "instance_id": int,                     # BandWacht instance ID
    "event_id": int,                        # Database event ID
    "timestamp": str,                       # ISO format timestamp
    "freq_hz": float,                       # Frequency in Hz
    "peak_db": float,                       # Peak power in dB
    "bandwidth_hz": float,                  # Bandwidth in Hz
    "duration_s": float,                    # Duration in seconds
    "target_label": str,                    # Target label/name
}
```

**Note:** The instance name/location is NOT included in the WebSocket event data. Only the `instance_id` is provided to identify which BandWacht instance produced the event.

---

### 4. BandWacht Class Constructor - Parameters

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` (lines 615-683)

```python
class BandWacht:
    """
    Main band monitoring class.
    Connects to an OpenWebRX instance and monitors for carriers.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        url: str,
        targets: list[WatchTarget] = None,
        scan_full_band: bool = False,
        threshold_db: float = -55.0,
        hysteresis_db: float = 5.0,
        hold_time_s: float = 2.0,
        cooldown_s: float = 10.0,
        notifiers: list[NotificationBackend] = None,
        record: bool = False,
        recording_dir: str = "./recordings",
        log_csv: bool = False,
        csv_file: str = "./bandwacht_log.csv",
        desired_profile: Optional[str] = None,
    ):
```

**Parameters accepted:**
1. **url** (str, required): OpenWebRX server URL (e.g., `http://my-webrx:8073`)
2. **targets** (list[WatchTarget], default=None): List of frequency targets to monitor
3. **scan_full_band** (bool, default=False): Enable full band scanning mode
4. **threshold_db** (float, default=-55.0): Detection threshold in dB
5. **hysteresis_db** (float, default=5.0): Hysteresis in dB for active/inactive transitions
6. **hold_time_s** (float, default=2.0): Hold time before trigger in seconds
7. **cooldown_s** (float, default=10.0): Cooldown between triggers in seconds
8. **notifiers** (list[NotificationBackend], default=None): List of notification backends
9. **record** (bool, default=False): Enable audio recording on detection
10. **recording_dir** (str, default="./recordings"): Output directory for recordings
11. **log_csv** (bool, default=False): Enable CSV logging of detections
12. **csv_file** (str, default="./bandwacht_log.csv"): CSV output file path
13. **desired_profile** (Optional[str], default=None): OpenWebRX profile ID to switch to (e.g., `rtlsdr|2m`)

---

### 5. ConsoleNotification.send() - Comparison

**File:** `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` (lines 219-230)

```python
class ConsoleNotification(NotificationBackend):
    """Print notifications to console."""

    async def send(self, event: DetectionEvent) -> bool:
        freq_mhz = event.freq_hz / 1e6
        label = f" [{event.target_label}]" if event.target_label else ""
        logger.info(
            f"🔴 CARRIER DETECTED{label}: {freq_mhz:.4f} MHz "
            f"| Peak: {event.peak_db:.1f} dB "
            f"| BW: {event.bandwidth_hz/1000:.1f} kHz"
        )
        return True
```

**Comparison with TelegramNotification:**

| Aspect | Console | Telegram |
|--------|---------|----------|
| **Output method** | Logger (stdout/file) | Telegram Bot API |
| **Message format** | Single-line with pipes | Multi-line with Markdown |
| **Emoji** | 🔴 Red dot | 📡 Satellite |
| **Fields included** | Frequency (MHz), Peak (dB), Bandwidth (kHz) | Frequency (MHz), Peak (dB), Time |
| **Language** | English | German ("Träger erkannt") |
| **Target label** | Included if present | Included if present |
| **Instance name** | NOT included | NOT included |
| **Timezone** | Not shown | Included in timestamp |
| **Bandwidth display** | Shown in kHz | NOT shown |
| **Asyncio** | Yes (async) | Yes (async) |

**Key differences:**
- ConsoleNotification shows **bandwidth**, TelegramNotification shows **timestamp with timezone**
- ConsoleNotification uses English, TelegramNotification uses German
- ConsoleNotification is simpler/lighter, TelegramNotification requires network calls
- Both inherit from `NotificationBackend` abstract base class
- Neither notification system includes the OpenWebRX instance name or location

---

### Summary

The notification and event systems in BandWacht are designed to be modular with pluggable backends. The `DetectionEvent` dataclass carries all detection metadata. Importantly, **neither the instance name nor location information is included in notifications or WebSocket broadcasts** - only the `target_label` from the watch target and the `instance_id` (for web scenarios) are used for identification. This means notifications are minimal and focused on the detection details (frequency, power, time) rather than broader context about which OpenWebRX instance they came from.

## BandWacht Targets System - Complete End-to-End Analysis

I have thoroughly explored the BandWacht project and understand the complete target workflow. Here's my comprehensive report:

---

### 1. DATABASE MODEL - WatchTarget (SQLAlchemy)
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/models.py` (lines 34-49)

```python
class WatchTarget(Base):
    __tablename__ = "watch_targets"

    id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, primary_key=True)
    instance_id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, ForeignKey("sdr_instances.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False)
    freq_hz: Mapped[float] = mapped_column(Float, nullable=False)
    bandwidth_hz: Mapped[float] = mapped_column(Float, default=12000.0)
    label: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(255), default="")
    threshold_db: Mapped[float] = mapped_column(Float, default=-55.0)
    enabled: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, default=True)
    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), default=_utcnow)
    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), default=_utcnow, onupdate=_utcnow)

    instance: Mapped["SdrInstance"] = relationship(back_populates="targets")
    events: Mapped[list["DetectionEvent"]] = relationship(back_populates="target", cascade="all, delete-orphan")
```

**Key attributes:**
- `instance_id`: Foreign key to SdrInstance (cascade delete)
- `freq_hz`: Center frequency in Hz (float)
- `bandwidth_hz`: Watch bandwidth (default 12 kHz)
- `label`: Display name/description
- `threshold_db`: Detection threshold (default -55 dB)
- `enabled`: Whether this target is active
- Timestamps and relationships for tracking events

**SdrInstance relationship** (lines 15-32):
```python
class SdrInstance(Base):
    __tablename__ = "sdr_instances"
    
    id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, primary_key=True)
    name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(255), nullable=False)
    url: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(512), nullable=False)
    enabled: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, default=True)
    is_connected: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, default=False)
    center_freq: Mapped[float | None] = mapped_column(Float, nullable=True)
    bandwidth: Mapped[float | None] = mapped_column(Float, nullable=True)
    fft_size: Mapped[int | None] = mapped_column(Integer, nullable=True)
    desired_profile: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(255), nullable=True)
    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), default=_utcnow)
    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), default=_utcnow, onupdate=_utcnow)

    targets: Mapped[list["WatchTarget"]] = relationship(back_populates="instance", cascade="all, delete-orphan")
    events: Mapped[list["DetectionEvent"]] = relationship(back_populates="instance", cascade="all, delete-orphan")
```

**NOTE on location/grid**: The SdrInstance model does **NOT** have location or grid fields. Only URL configuration.

---

### 2. PYDANTIC SCHEMAS (Request/Response Models)
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/schemas.py` (lines 50-75)

```python
class TargetBase(BaseModel):
    instance_id: int
    freq_hz: float
    bandwidth_hz: float = 12000.0
    label: str = ""
    threshold_db: float = -55.0
    enabled: bool = True

class TargetCreate(TargetBase):
    pass

class TargetUpdate(BaseModel):
    freq_hz: float | None = None
    bandwidth_hz: float | None = None
    label: str | None = None
    threshold_db: float | None = None
    enabled: bool | None = None

class TargetRead(TargetBase):
    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)
    id: int
    created_at: datetime
    updated_at: datetime
```

---

### 3. CRUD OPERATIONS
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/crud.py` (lines 73-113)

```python
async def get_targets(db: AsyncSession, instance_id: int | None = None) -> list[WatchTarget]:
    q = select(WatchTarget).order_by(WatchTarget.id)
    if instance_id is not None:
        q = q.where(WatchTarget.instance_id == instance_id)
    result = await db.execute(q)
    return list(result.scalars().all())

async def get_target(db: AsyncSession, target_id: int) -> WatchTarget | None:
    return await db.get(WatchTarget, target_id)

async def create_target(db: AsyncSession, data: TargetCreate) -> WatchTarget:
    target = WatchTarget(**data.model_dump())
    db.add(target)
    await db.commit()
    await db.refresh(target)
    return target

async def update_target(db: AsyncSession, target_id: int, data: TargetUpdate) -> WatchTarget | None:
    target = await db.get(WatchTarget, target_id)
    if not target:
        return None
    for key, val in data.model_dump(exclude_unset=True).items():
        setattr(target, key, val)
    await db.commit()
    await db.refresh(target)
    return target

async def delete_target(db: AsyncSession, target_id: int) -> bool:
    target = await db.get(WatchTarget, target_id)
    if not target:
        return False
    await db.delete(target)
    await db.commit()
    return True
```

router = APIRouter()

@router.get("/targets", response_model=list[TargetRead])
async def list_targets(
    instance_id: int | None = Query(None),
    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db),
):
    return await crud.get_targets(db, instance_id=instance_id)

@router.post("/targets", response_model=TargetRead, status_code=201)
async def create_target(data: TargetCreate, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
    inst = await crud.get_instance(db, data.instance_id)
    if not inst:
        raise HTTPException(404, "SDR-Instanz nicht gefunden")
    return await crud.create_target(db, data)

@router.get("/targets/{target_id}", response_model=TargetRead)
async def get_target(target_id: int, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
    target = await crud.get_target(db, target_id)
    if not target:
        raise HTTPException(404, "Ziel nicht gefunden")
    return target

@router.put("/targets/{target_id}", response_model=TargetRead)
async def update_target(target_id: int, data: TargetUpdate, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
    target = await crud.update_target(db, target_id, data)
    if not target:
        raise HTTPException(404, "Ziel nicht gefunden")
    return target

@router.delete("/targets/{target_id}")
async def delete_target(target_id: int, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
    if not await crud.delete_target(db, target_id):
        raise HTTPException(404, "Ziel nicht gefunden")
    return {"ok": True}

- `DELETE /api/v1/targets/{target_id}` - Delete target

### 5. TARGET LOADING IN START_INSTANCE (MonitorManager)
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` (lines 155-179)

```python
async def start_instance(self, instance_id: int, db: AsyncSession):
    if self.is_running(instance_id):
        return

    inst = await crud.get_instance(db, instance_id)
    if not inst:
        raise ValueError(f"Instance {instance_id} not found")

    # CRITICAL: Load targets from database
    targets_db = await crud.get_targets(db, instance_id=instance_id)
    settings = await crud.get_settings(db)

    # Convert DB targets to WatchTarget objects
    watch_targets = []
    target_map: dict[float, int] = {}
    for t in targets_db:
        if not t.enabled:  # Skip disabled targets
            continue
        wt = WatchTarget(
            freq_hz=t.freq_hz,
            bandwidth_hz=t.bandwidth_hz,
            label=t.label,
            threshold_db=t.threshold_db,
        )
        watch_targets.append(wt)
        target_map[t.freq_hz] = t.id  # Map for event tracking

    # ... Build notifiers, create monitor, start task ...
    monitor = WebBandWacht(
        url=inst.url,
        targets=watch_targets,  # Targets passed here
        scan_full_band=settings.scan_full_band,
        threshold_db=settings.threshold_db,
        hysteresis_db=settings.hysteresis_db,
        hold_time_s=settings.hold_time_s,
        cooldown_s=settings.cooldown_s,
        notifiers=notifiers,
        record=settings.record_enabled,
        desired_profile=inst.desired_profile,
        on_fft_callback=on_fft,
        on_connected_callback=on_connected,
    )
```

**Key Flow**:
1. Load instance from DB
2. Load all enabled targets for that instance from DB
3. Convert DB WatchTarget objects to bandwacht.WatchTarget objects
4. Create target_map to track freq_hz → DB target id mapping
5. Pass targets to WebBandWacht constructor
6. Notifier captures detection events and saves them with target_id from target_map

---

### 6. DATABASE NOTIFIER - Event Capture
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` (lines 36-72)

```python
class DatabaseNotifier(NotificationBackend):
    """Captures detection events into the database and broadcasts to WebSocket clients."""

    def __init__(self, instance_id: int, target_map: dict[float, int],
                 on_event: Optional[Callable] = None):
        self.instance_id = instance_id
        self.target_map = target_map  # freq_hz -> target db id
        self.on_event = on_event

    async def send(self, event: DetectionEvent) -> bool:
        target_id = self.target_map.get(event.freq_hz)  # Lookup target by frequency
        async with async_session() as db:
            db_event = await crud.create_event(
                db,
                instance_id=self.instance_id,
                target_id=target_id,  # Link event to target
                timestamp=event.timestamp,
                freq_hz=event.freq_hz,
                peak_db=event.peak_db,
                bandwidth_hz=event.bandwidth_hz,
                duration_s=event.duration_s,
                target_label=event.target_label,
                recording_file=event.recording_file,
            )
            if self.on_event:
                await self.on_event({
                    "type": "detection",
                    "instance_id": self.instance_id,
                    "event_id": db_event.id,
                    "timestamp": event.timestamp.isoformat(),
                    "freq_hz": event.freq_hz,
                    "peak_db": event.peak_db,
                    "bandwidth_hz": event.bandwidth_hz,
                    "duration_s": event.duration_s,
                    "target_label": event.target_label,
                })
        return True
```

export default function ConfigPage() {
  const [instances, setInstances] = useState<SdrInstance[]>([])
  const [allTargets, setAllTargets] = useState<WatchTarget[]>([])

  const refreshInstances = useCallback(async () => {
    const data = await instancesApi.list()
    setInstances(data)
  }, [])

  const refreshTargets = useCallback(async () => {
    const data = await targetsApi.list()
    setAllTargets(data)
  }, [])

  const refreshAll = useCallback(() => {
    refreshInstances()
    refreshTargets()
  }, [refreshInstances, refreshTargets])

  useEffect(() => { refreshAll() }, [refreshAll])

  return (
    <div className="space-y-6">
      {/* Instance management */}
      <div className="card">
        <div className="flex items-center gap-2 mb-4">
          <Radio className="w-4 h-4 text-sdr-cyan" />

export default function TargetList({ targets, instances, onRefresh }: TargetListProps) {
  const [showForm, setShowForm] = useState(false)
  const [editing, setEditing] = useState<WatchTarget | null>(null)
  const [deleteId, setDeleteId] = useState<number | null>(null)

  const handleDelete = async () => {
    if (deleteId == null) return
    await api.delete(deleteId)
    setDeleteId(null)
    onRefresh()
  }

  const getInstanceName = (id: number) =>
    instances.find(i => i.id === id)?.name ?? `#${id}`

  return (
    <>
      <div className="flex items-center justify-between mb-3">
        <h4 className="text-sm font-medium">{UI.cfg_targets}</h4>
        <button
          onClick={() => { setEditing(null); setShowForm(true) }}
          className="btn-primary flex items-center gap-1.5"
          disabled={instances.length === 0}
        >
          <Plus className="w-3.5 h-3.5" />
          {UI.cfg_add_target}
        </button>
      </div>

      {targets.length === 0 ? (
        <p className="text-sdr-muted text-sm py-4 text-center">
          {instances.length === 0 ? 'Zuerst eine SDR-Instanz anlegen.' : 'Keine Überwachungsziele konfiguriert.'}
        </p>
      ) : (
        <div className="space-y-2">
          {targets.map(t => (
            <div key={t.id} className="flex items-center justify-between p-3 bg-sdr-bg rounded-lg border border-sdr-border">
              <div className="flex items-center gap-3 min-w-0">
                <StatusBadge active={t.enabled} />
                <div className="min-w-0">
                  <p className="text-sm font-medium truncate">
                    {t.label || formatFreqMHz(t.freq_hz)}
                  </p>
                  <p className="text-xs text-sdr-muted">
                    {formatFreqMHz(t.freq_hz)} &middot; {(t.bandwidth_hz / 1000).toFixed(1)} kHz &middot; {formatDb(t.threshold_db)} &middot; {getInstanceName(t.instance_id)}
                  </p>
                </div>
              </div>
              <div className="flex items-center gap-1 shrink-0">
                <button onClick={() => { setEditing(t); setShowForm(true) }} className="p-1.5 text-sdr-muted hover:text-sdr-text transition-colors">
                  <Pencil className="w-3.5 h-3.5" />
                </button>
                <button onClick={() => setDeleteId(t.id)} className="p-1.5 text-sdr-muted hover:text-sdr-red transition-colors">
                  <Trash2 className="w-3.5 h-3.5" />
                </button>
              </div>
            </div>
          ))}
        </div>
      )}

      {showForm && (
        <TargetForm
          target={editing}
          instances={instances}
          onClose={() => setShowForm(false)}
          onSaved={() => { setShowForm(false); onRefresh() }}
        />
      )}

      <ConfirmDialog
        open={deleteId != null}
        message={UI.confirm_delete}
        onConfirm={handleDelete}
        onCancel={() => setDeleteId(null)}
      />
    </>
  )
}

### 9. FRONTEND - TARGET FORM COMPONENT
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/frontend/src/components/config/TargetForm.tsx`

```typescript
export default function TargetForm({ target, instances, onClose, onSaved }: TargetFormProps) {
  const [instanceId, setInstanceId] = useState(target?.instance_id ?? instances[0]?.id ?? 0)
  const [freqHz, setFreqHz] = useState(target?.freq_hz ?? 145_500_000)
  const [bandwidthHz, setBandwidthHz] = useState(target?.bandwidth_hz ?? 12000)
  const [label, setLabel] = useState(target?.label ?? '')
  const [thresholdDb, setThresholdDb] = useState(target?.threshold_db ?? -55)
  const [enabled, setEnabled] = useState(target?.enabled ?? true)
  const [saving, setSaving] = useState(false)
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null)

  const handleSubmit = async (e: React.FormEvent) => {
    e.preventDefault()
    setSaving(true)
    setError(null)
    try {
      if (target) {
        await api.update(target.id, {
          freq_hz: freqHz,
          bandwidth_hz: bandwidthHz,
          label,
          threshold_db: thresholdDb,
          enabled,
        })
      } else {
        await api.create({
          instance_id: instanceId,
          freq_hz: freqHz,
          bandwidth_hz: bandwidthHz,
          label,
          threshold_db: thresholdDb,
          enabled,
        })
      }
      onSaved()
    } catch (err: any) {
      setError(err.message)
    } finally {
      setSaving(false)
    }
  }

  return (
    <div className="fixed inset-0 bg-black/60 z-50 flex items-center justify-center p-4">
      <form onSubmit={handleSubmit} className="card max-w-md w-full space-y-4">
        <div className="flex items-center justify-between">
          <h3 className="text-sm font-medium">
            {target ? 'Ziel bearbeiten' : UI.cfg_add_target}
          </h3>
          <button type="button" onClick={onClose} className="text-sdr-muted hover:text-sdr-text">
            <X className="w-4 h-4" />
          </button>
        </div>

        {error && <p className="text-sdr-red text-xs">{error}</p>}

        {!target && (
          <div>
            <label className="label">SDR-Instanz</label>
            <select className="input w-full" value={instanceId} onChange={e => setInstanceId(Number(e.target.value))}>
              {instances.map(i => (
                <option key={i.id} value={i.id}>{i.name}</option>
              ))}
            </select>
          </div>
        )}

        <FrequencyInput value={freqHz} onChange={setFreqHz} label={UI.cfg_freq} />

        <div>
          <label className="label">{UI.cfg_bandwidth}</label>
          <div className="relative">
            <input
              type="number"
              step="0.1"
              value={bandwidthHz / 1000}
              onChange={e => setBandwidthHz(parseFloat(e.target.value) * 1000)}
              className="input w-full pr-12"
            />
            <span className="absolute right-3 top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 text-xs text-sdr-muted">kHz</span>
          </div>
        </div>

        <div>
          <label className="label">{UI.cfg_label}</label>
          <input className="input w-full" value={label} onChange={e => setLabel(e.target.value)} placeholder="z.B. OE8XKK Ausgabe" />
        </div>

        <DecibelSlider value={thresholdDb} onChange={setThresholdDb} label={UI.cfg_threshold} />

        <label className="flex items-center gap-2 text-sm cursor-pointer">
          <input type="checkbox" checked={enabled} onChange={e => setEnabled(e.target.checked)} className="accent-sdr-cyan" />
          {UI.cfg_enabled}
        </label>

        <div className="flex justify-end gap-2 pt-2">
          <button type="button" onClick={onClose} className="btn-ghost">{UI.cfg_cancel}</button>
          <button type="submit" disabled={saving} className="btn-primary">
            {saving ? UI.loading : UI.cfg_save}
          </button>
        </div>
      </form>
    </div>
  )
}
```

**Input fields**:
- SDR Instance (dropdown, for new targets only)
- Frequency (Hz)
- Bandwidth (kHz, converted to Hz)
- Label (text)
- Threshold (dB slider)
- Enabled (checkbox)

      method: 'PUT',

}

### 12. BANDWACHT CORE - WatchTarget Class
**File**: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` (core library)

From the bandwacht library (imported by monitor_manager.py):

```python
@dataclass
class WatchTarget:
    """A frequency or frequency range to monitor."""
    freq_hz: float                # Center frequency in Hz
    bandwidth_hz: float = 12000   # Watch bandwidth (default 12kHz)
    label: str = ""
    threshold_db: float = -55.0
    # State tracking
    active: bool = False
    active_since: Optional[float] = None
    peak_db: float = -120.0
    last_seen: Optional[float] = None

    @property
    def low_hz(self):
        return self.freq_hz - self.bandwidth_hz / 2

    @property
    def high_hz(self):
        return self.freq_hz + self.bandwidth_hz / 2
```

---

## COMPLETE TARGET WORKFLOW

**1. CREATION**:
   - User fills form in UI (TargetForm)
   - Frontend calls `POST /api/v1/targets` with instance_id, freq_hz, bandwidth_hz, label, threshold_db, enabled
   - Backend validates instance exists
   - CRUD creates WatchTarget record in DB
   - UI refreshes target list

**2. LOADING (Instance startup)**:
   - Start button clicked in UI
   - Backend calls `manager.start_instance(instance_id, db)`
   - MonitorManager loads all enabled targets from DB via `crud.get_targets(db, instance_id)`
   - For each enabled target, creates bandwacht.WatchTarget object
   - Builds target_map: freq_hz → DB target id
   - Passes targets list to WebBandWacht constructor

**3. MONITORING (Runtime)**:
   - WebBandWacht receives FFT data from OpenWebRX
   - SpectrumAnalyzer.analyze_band(fft_data, targets) checks each target
   - When signal detected at target frequency above threshold:
     - Creates DetectionEvent with freq_hz, peak_db, etc.
     - DatabaseNotifier.send() captures event:
       - Looks up target_id from target_map[freq_hz]
       - Creates DB detection_event with target_id linking
       - Broadcasts to WebSocket clients

**4. UPDATING/DELETING**:
   - User edits/deletes in UI
   - Frontend calls `PUT /targets/{id}` or `DELETE /targets/{id}`
   - Backend updates/deletes DB record
   - **Note**: Does NOT restart monitoring automatically
   - Instance must be restarted to reflect changes

---

## KEY FINDINGS

- **Database**: WatchTarget has 8 fields (id, instance_id, freq_hz, bandwidth_hz, label, threshold_db, enabled, timestamps)
- **Location field**: **NONE** - SdrInstance only has URL configuration, no location/grid field
- **Frequency units**: Hz internally (converted from MHz in UI)
- **Bandwidth**: Default 12 kHz, stored in Hz
- **Threshold**: dB values, default -55
- **Enabled**: Boolean flag to skip disabled targets during startup
- **Target linking**: Events linked by freq_hz mapping during initialization (target_map)
- **Frontend**: React components with modal form for create/edit, list with status badges
- **API**: RESTful endpoints with JWT auth protection

Now I have a thorough understanding of the entire codebase. Let me present the detailed implementation plan.

---

## Detailed Implementation Plan: Global Targets and Instance Location

### Design Decision Recommendations

**Decision 1: How to pass instance name/grid to notification backends?**

**Recommendation: Option (a) -- Add `instance_name` and `instance_grid` to the core `DetectionEvent` dataclass with empty-string defaults.**

Rationale: This is the simplest approach, fully backwards-compatible (existing CLI usage continues to work with default empty strings), and requires no wrapper pattern. The core `DetectionEvent` in `bandwacht.py` gains two optional fields. The `DatabaseNotifier` in `monitor_manager.py` already has `self.instance_id` and can easily look up name/grid from the DB row it already holds. Each notification backend (`TelegramNotification`, `ConsoleNotification`, etc.) simply checks if `instance_name` is non-empty and includes it in the message. No new classes or architectural indirection are needed.

**Decision 2: How to handle global targets in the DB schema?**

**Recommendation: Option (a) -- Make `instance_id` nullable on `WatchTarget` (NULL = global).**

Rationale: A separate `GlobalTarget` table would duplicate the entire schema and require parallel CRUD. Making `instance_id` nullable is a single-column change. The FK relationship with `SdrInstance` already exists; making it nullable only means "this target is unbound." The ORM `back_populates="targets"` relationship on `SdrInstance` naturally excludes NULL-instance targets, so the existing `targets` relationship on `SdrInstance` is unaffected. The query in `crud.get_targets` already supports filtering by `instance_id`; we just need a new query method (or parameter) to fetch global targets (WHERE instance_id IS NULL).

**Decision 3: How to seed the default 2m simplex frequencies?**

**Recommendation: Option (b) -- Seed in the `lifespan` function on first startup if no global targets exist.**

Rationale: This runs automatically when the web app starts, requires no separate management commands, and is idempotent (only seeds if no global targets exist). The user can delete the seeded targets later if unwanted. A management command would be easily forgotten; an API endpoint would require manual invocation.

### File-by-File Implementation Plan

#### Phase 1: Core Data Model Changes

**File 1: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py`** (lines 131-142)

Add `instance_name` and `instance_grid` to the `DetectionEvent` dataclass:

```python

    bandwidth_hz: float
    duration_s: float = 0.0
    target_label: str = ""
    recording_file: Optional[str] = None
    instance_name: str = ""       # NEW
    instance_grid: str = ""       # NEW
```

These fields have empty-string defaults, so all existing construction sites (CLI, test notifications, etc.) remain unchanged.

Update notification backends to include instance info when present:

- `ConsoleNotification.send()` (line ~222-229): Prepend instance name/grid to the log line if non-empty. For example: `f" @ {event.instance_name} ({event.instance_grid})"` appended if either is truthy.

- `TelegramNotification.send()` (line ~273-296): Add a line like `Empfanger: {instance_name} ({instance_grid})` to the message text when instance_name is non-empty.

- `GotifyNotification.send()` (line ~241-263): Same pattern -- add instance info line to the message body.

- `NtfyNotification.send()` (line ~306-328): Same pattern.

- `WebhookNotification.send()` (line ~337-357): Add `"instance_name"` and `"instance_grid"` to the JSON payload.

The pattern for each backend is identical: after constructing the `label` variable, build an `instance_info` string:

```python
instance_info = ""
if event.instance_name:
    instance_info = f"\nEmpfanger: {event.instance_name}"
    if event.instance_grid:
        instance_info += f" ({event.instance_grid})"
```

Then include `instance_info` in the message string.
