#### Phase 2: Database Model + Schema Changes

**File 2: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/models.py`**

Two changes:

**(a) Add `grid_locator` to `SdrInstance`** (after line 26):

```python
grid_locator: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(10), nullable=True)
```

**(b) Make `instance_id` nullable on `WatchTarget`** (line 38):

Change from:
```python
instance_id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, ForeignKey("sdr_instances.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False)
```
To:
```python
instance_id: Mapped[int | None] = mapped_column(Integer, ForeignKey("sdr_instances.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=True)
```

Also update the relationship on line 47 to handle the nullable FK properly:
```python
instance: Mapped["SdrInstance | None"] = relationship(back_populates="targets")
```

**Important note on the `SdrInstance.targets` relationship (line 30):** The `cascade="all, delete-orphan"` will cause an issue: when an instance is deleted, global targets (instance_id=NULL) are not owned by any instance, so they are fine. But the existing relationship `back_populates="targets"` on `SdrInstance` only returns targets for that instance. However, `delete-orphan` with a nullable FK can cause unexpected behavior -- SQLAlchemy may try to treat global targets as orphans. The fix: change the `SdrInstance.targets` relationship to use a `primaryjoin` that explicitly filters on the FK, and remove `delete-orphan` from cascade. Instead, use `cascade="all"` and let the database `ON DELETE CASCADE` handle instance-specific cleanup. Or alternatively, keep the code and accept that `delete-orphan` only applies when a target is removed from an instance's collection, not on deletion of the instance itself (the DB `ON DELETE CASCADE` handles that). Testing required here.

**Database migration strategy:** Since Alembic is not used and `init_db()` uses `Base.metadata.create_all()`, SQLite will NOT add new columns to existing tables. The approach is:

1. Stop the app
2. Use a SQL migration script to `ALTER TABLE sdr_instances ADD COLUMN grid_locator VARCHAR(10)` and `ALTER TABLE watch_targets ALTER COLUMN instance_id` -- but SQLite does not support `ALTER COLUMN`. 

For SQLite, making a NOT NULL column nullable requires recreating the table. The recommended approach:

```sql
-- For watch_targets: recreate with nullable instance_id
CREATE TABLE watch_targets_new (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    instance_id INTEGER REFERENCES sdr_instances(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    freq_hz REAL NOT NULL,
    bandwidth_hz REAL DEFAULT 12000.0,
    label VARCHAR(255) DEFAULT '',
    threshold_db REAL DEFAULT -55.0,
    enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT 1,
    created_at DATETIME,
    updated_at DATETIME
);
INSERT INTO watch_targets_new SELECT * FROM watch_targets;
DROP TABLE watch_targets;
ALTER TABLE watch_targets_new RENAME TO watch_targets;

-- For sdr_instances: just add the column (simpler)
ALTER TABLE sdr_instances ADD COLUMN grid_locator VARCHAR(10);
```

Provide this as a migration script (e.g., `migrate_v2.sql`) or embed it in a Python startup function. Alternatively, for a fresh deployment, deleting the DB file and letting `create_all()` regenerate it is acceptable if data loss is OK.

#### Phase 3: CRUD Layer Changes

**File 4: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/crud.py`**

**(a) New function to get global targets:**

```python
async def get_global_targets(db: AsyncSession) -> list[WatchTarget]:
    """Get all targets with instance_id IS NULL (global)."""
    q = select(WatchTarget).where(WatchTarget.instance_id.is_(None)).order_by(WatchTarget.id)
    result = await db.execute(q)
    return list(result.scalars().all())
```

**(b) New function to get targets for an instance INCLUDING globals:**

```python
async def get_targets_for_instance(db: AsyncSession, instance_id: int) -> list[WatchTarget]:
    """Get instance-specific targets AND all global targets."""
    from sqlalchemy import or_
    q = select(WatchTarget).where(
        or_(
            WatchTarget.instance_id == instance_id,
            WatchTarget.instance_id.is_(None)
        )
    ).order_by(WatchTarget.id)
    result = await db.execute(q)
    return list(result.scalars().all())
```

**(c) Update `create_target`** to handle `instance_id=None`:

The current `create_target` in `routers/targets.py` validates that `instance_id` points to a valid instance. When `instance_id` is None, skip that validation. The CRUD function itself is fine (it does `WatchTarget(**data.model_dump())`); the router needs the logic change.

**(d) Seed function** -- add a new function:

```python
async def seed_global_targets(db: AsyncSession) -> int:
    """Seed default 2m simplex frequencies as global targets if none exist.
    Returns number of targets created."""
    existing = await get_global_targets(db)
    if existing:
        return 0  # Already seeded

    DEFAULT_2M_TARGETS = [
        # FM Simplex channels
        (145_300_000, 12500, "S12 FM Simplex 145.300"),
        (145_337_500, 12500, "S14 FM Simplex 145.3375"),
        (145_375_000, 12500, "S16 Digital Voice Calling"),
        (145_412_500, 12500, "S18 FM Simplex 145.4125"),
        (145_450_000, 12500, "S20 FM Simplex 145.450"),
        (145_475_000, 12500, "S21 FM Simplex 145.475"),
        (145_500_000, 12500, "S22 FM Anruf / Calling"),
        (145_525_000, 12500, "S23 FM Simplex 145.525"),
        (145_550_000, 12500, "FM Simplex 145.550"),
        (145_575_000, 12500, "FM Simplex 145.575"),
        (145_600_000, 12500, "FM Simplex 145.600"),
        # SSB / Weak Signal
        (144_174_000, 3000, "FT8 144.174"),
        (144_300_000, 3000, "SSB Anruf / Calling 144.300"),
        # Special
        (144_800_000, 12500, "APRS 144.800"),
        (145_800_000, 12500, "ISS 145.800"),
    ]

    count = 0
    for freq_hz, bw_hz, label in DEFAULT_2M_TARGETS:
        target = WatchTarget(
            instance_id=None,
            freq_hz=freq_hz,
            bandwidth_hz=bw_hz,
            label=label,
            threshold_db=-55.0,
            enabled=True,
        )
        db.add(target)
        count += 1

    await db.commit()
    return count
```

Note on 25 kHz channel spacing: S12=145.300, S14=145.3375 (12.5 kHz sub-spacing in IARU Region 1), S16=145.375, etc. The exact channel plan for Austrian/IARU R1 should be verified, but the user specified "S12, S14, S16, S18, S20, S21, S22, S23" which are these frequencies. The 25 kHz spacing mentioned in the requirements refers to the FM channel grid, but actual IARU R1 uses 12.5 kHz sub-channels within 25 kHz spacing. The monitoring bandwidth of 12.5 kHz per target is correct for narrowband FM.

@router.post("/targets", response_model=TargetRead, status_code=201)
async def create_target(data: TargetCreate, db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db)):
    if data.instance_id is not None:
        inst = await crud.get_instance(db, data.instance_id)
        if not inst:
            raise HTTPException(404, "SDR-Instanz nicht gefunden")
    return await crud.create_target(db, data)
```

Optionally add a query parameter to the `list_targets` endpoint to filter global-only targets:

```python
@router.get("/targets", response_model=list[TargetRead])
async def list_targets(
    instance_id: int | None = Query(None),
    global_only: bool = Query(False),
    db: AsyncSession = Depends(get_db),
):
    if global_only:
        return await crud.get_global_targets(db)
    return await crud.get_targets(db, instance_id=instance_id)
```

**(b) `DatabaseNotifier`**: Enrich the `DetectionEvent` with instance name/grid before passing to the on_event callback and other notifiers. Currently `DatabaseNotifier.__init__` receives `instance_id`. Change it to also receive `instance_name` and `instance_grid`:

```python
class DatabaseNotifier(NotificationBackend):
    def __init__(self, instance_id: int, instance_name: str, instance_grid: str,
                 target_map: dict[float, int], on_event: Optional[Callable] = None):
        self.instance_id = instance_id
        self.instance_name = instance_name
        self.instance_grid = instance_grid
        self.target_map = target_map
        self.on_event = on_event

    async def send(self, event: DetectionEvent) -> bool:
        # Enrich event with instance info
        event.instance_name = self.instance_name
        event.instance_grid = self.instance_grid
        # ... rest unchanged ...
```

Update the construction in `start_instance`:

```python
db_notifier = DatabaseNotifier(
    instance_id=instance_id,
    instance_name=inst.name,
    instance_grid=inst.grid_locator or "",
    target_map=target_map,
    on_event=self._broadcast_event,
)
```

This is the key mechanism: the `DatabaseNotifier.send()` is called first (it is the first notifier in the list), and it mutates `event.instance_name` and `event.instance_grid` before the event is passed to subsequent notifiers (`ConsoleNotification`, `TelegramNotification`, etc.) in `BandWacht._notify_all()`. Wait -- actually, looking at the code in `bandwacht.py` line 779-785:

```python
async def _notify_all(self, event: DetectionEvent):
    for notifier in self.notifiers:
        try:
            await notifier.send(event)
```

The notifiers are called sequentially in order, and the same `event` object is passed to each. So if `DatabaseNotifier` mutates `event.instance_name` first, all subsequent notifiers will see it. This works because `DatabaseNotifier` is always the first notifier added (line 183-189 of monitor_manager.py). This is a clean approach.

```

#### Phase 6: Application Startup (Seeding)

**File 8: `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/main.py`**

Add seeding after `init_db()` in the `lifespan` function:

```python
@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
    await init_db()
    # Seed default global targets if none exist
    await _seed_global_targets()
    # Auto-start all enabled instances
    await _autostart_instances()
    yield
    from .services.monitor_manager import manager
    await manager.stop_all()


async def _seed_global_targets():
    import logging
    from . import crud
    from .database import async_session
    logger = logging.getLogger("bandwacht.web")
    try:
        async with async_session() as db:
            count = await crud.seed_global_targets(db)
            if count:
                logger.info(f"Seeded {count} default global 2m simplex targets")
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Failed to seed global targets: {e}")
```

#### Phase 8: Database Migration Script

Create a migration script `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/migrate_v2_global_targets.py`:

```python
"""
Migration script: Add grid_locator to sdr_instances, make watch_targets.instance_id nullable.
Run this BEFORE starting the updated application.

Usage: python migrate_v2_global_targets.py
"""
import sqlite3
import sys

DB_PATH = "bandwacht_web.db"

def migrate():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH)
    c = conn.cursor()
    
    # 1. Add grid_locator to sdr_instances
    try:
        c.execute("ALTER TABLE sdr_instances ADD COLUMN grid_locator VARCHAR(10)")
        print("Added grid_locator column to sdr_instances")
    except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
        if "duplicate column" in str(e).lower():
            print("grid_locator column already exists")
        else:
            raise
    
    # 2. Recreate watch_targets with nullable instance_id
    c.execute("SELECT sql FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='watch_targets'")
    create_sql = c.fetchone()
    if create_sql and "NOT NULL" in create_sql[0] and "instance_id" in create_sql[0]:
        print("Recreating watch_targets with nullable instance_id...")
        c.execute("""
            CREATE TABLE watch_targets_new (
                id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
                instance_id INTEGER REFERENCES sdr_instances(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
                freq_hz REAL NOT NULL,
                bandwidth_hz REAL DEFAULT 12000.0,
                label VARCHAR(255) DEFAULT '',
                threshold_db REAL DEFAULT -55.0,
                enabled BOOLEAN DEFAULT 1,
                created_at DATETIME,
                updated_at DATETIME
            )
        """)
        c.execute("INSERT INTO watch_targets_new SELECT * FROM watch_targets")
        c.execute("DROP TABLE watch_targets")
        c.execute("ALTER TABLE watch_targets_new RENAME TO watch_targets")
        print("watch_targets recreated successfully")
    else:
        print("watch_targets already has nullable instance_id (or table not found)")
    
    # 3. Set known grid locators
    known_grids = {
        'OE8CNI': 'JN66UO',
        'OE8YML': 'JN66TO',
        'OE8CXC': 'JN66SS',
    }
    for name, grid in known_grids.items():
        c.execute("UPDATE sdr_instances SET grid_locator = ? WHERE name LIKE ?", (grid, f"%{name}%"))
        if c.rowcount:
            print(f"Set grid_locator={grid} for instance matching '{name}'")
    
    conn.commit()
    conn.close()
    print("Migration complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    migrate()
```

---

### Implementation Sequence

1. **Backend model + schema changes** (models.py, schemas.py) -- foundational
2. **Migration script** -- run against existing DB
3. **CRUD changes** (crud.py) -- new query functions + seed function  
4. **Core DetectionEvent changes** (bandwacht.py) -- add fields + update notifiers
5. **Monitor manager changes** (monitor_manager.py) -- enrichment + global target loading
6. **Router changes** (targets.py) -- allow null instance_id
7. **Startup seeding** (main.py) -- call seed on lifespan
8. **Frontend types + API client** (types/index.ts, client.ts)
9. **Frontend forms** (TargetForm.tsx, InstanceForm.tsx)
10. **Frontend lists** (TargetList.tsx, InstanceList.tsx)
11. **Frontend dashboard** (EventFeed.tsx, Dashboard.tsx)

### Potential Challenges

1. **SQLite migration** -- The table recreation for `watch_targets` is the riskiest step. Backing up the DB before running the migration script is essential.

2. **SQLAlchemy `delete-orphan` with nullable FK** -- The `SdrInstance.targets` relationship uses `cascade="all, delete-orphan"`. With nullable FKs, SQLAlchemy may raise errors when setting `instance_id=None` because it treats the target as an orphan. The fix is to change the cascade on `SdrInstance.targets` from `"all, delete-orphan"` to `"all"`. The database-level `ON DELETE CASCADE` will still clean up instance-specific targets when an instance is deleted.

3. **Global targets and the `target_map`** -- In `monitor_manager.py`, the `target_map` maps `freq_hz -> target_db_id` for the `DatabaseNotifier` to record which target triggered a detection. Global targets will have different DB IDs than instance-specific targets but the same frequency. Multiple instances sharing the same global target will correctly map back to the same target ID, which is correct behavior.

4. **Frontend backwards compatibility** -- The `TargetForm` currently defaults `instanceId` to `instances[0]?.id ?? 0`. Changing this to `null` means existing behavior where the first instance is pre-selected will change. For new targets, the user will now see "Global (alle Instanzen)" as the default, which is actually a better default given the feature intent.

5. **Notification message length** -- Adding instance name/grid to Telegram messages increases message length. This is unlikely to be an issue (Telegram supports up to 4096 characters) but worth noting.

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/bandwacht.py` - Core DetectionEvent dataclass and all notification backends to modify
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/models.py` - Database models: nullable instance_id on WatchTarget, grid_locator on SdrInstance
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` - Target loading (global + instance), event enrichment with instance name/grid
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/backend/crud.py` - New query functions (get_targets_for_instance, get_global_targets, seed_global_targets)
- `/home/achildrenmile/bandwacht/web/frontend/src/components/config/TargetForm.tsx` - Global target UI: optional instance selector with "Global" option

  "content": "# Plan: Global Targets, Maidenhead Grid, Instance in Notifications\n\n## Context\n\nTargets are currently per-instance — to monitor the same frequency on 3 SDRs you need 3 targets. This adds global targets (`instance_id = NULL`) that apply to all instances automatically. Also adds Maidenhead grid to SDR instances and includes instance name + grid in Telegram/console notifications so users know *where* a signal was detected.\n\n## Frequencies to seed (direct simplex only, no repeaters, no APRS)\n\n**FM simplex 145.300–145.600 MHz** (12.5 kHz BW):\n- 145.300 S12, 145.337 S13, 145.375 DV Anruf, 145.400 S16, 145.450 S18, 145.475 S19, 145.500 FM Anruf, 145.525 S21, 145.550 S22, 145.575 S23, 145.600 S24\n\n**SSB/weak-signal 144.150–144.400 MHz** (3 kHz BW):\n- 144.050 CW Anruf, 144.100 CW Meteorscatter, 144.174 FT8, 144.300 SSB Anruf, 144.370 MSK144\n\n**Special** (12.5 kHz BW):\n- 145.800 ISS Downlink\n\n## Changes\n\n### 1. `bandwacht.py` — DetectionEvent + notification backends\n\nAdd two fields (backwards-compat defaults):\n```python\ninstance_name: str = \"\"\ninstance_grid: str = \"\"\n```\n\nUpdate `TelegramNotification.send()` — add line `Empfänger: OE8CNI (JN66UO)` when set.\nUpdate `ConsoleNotification.send()` — append `@ OE8CNI (JN66UO)`.\nSame pattern for Gotify, Ntfy, Webhook.\n\n### 2. `web/backend/models.py` — schema changes\n\n- `SdrInstance`: add `grid_locator: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(10), nullable=True)`\n- `WatchTarget`: change `instance_id` from `NOT NULL` to **nullable** (`Mapped[int | None]`), update relationship to `Mapped[\"SdrInstance | None\"]`\n- `SdrInstance.targets` relationship: change cascade from `\"all, delete-orphan\"` to `\"all\"` (delete-orphan conflicts with nullable FK; DB-level ON DELETE CASCADE still cleans up)\n\n### 3. `web/backend/schemas.py`\n\n- `InstanceBase` + `InstanceUpdate` + `InstanceRead`: add `grid_locator: str | None = None`\n- `TargetBase`: change `instance_id: int` → `instance_id: int | None = None`\n\n### 4. `web/backend/crud.py` — new queries + seed\n\n- `get_global_targets(db)` — WHERE instance_id IS NULL\n- `get_targets_for_instance(db, instance_id)` — WHERE instance_id = ? OR instance_id IS NULL\n- `seed_global_targets(db)` — insert default 2m frequencies if no global targets exist\n\n### 5. `web/backend/routers/targets.py`\n\n- `create_target`: skip instance validation when `instance_id is None`\n\n### 6. `web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py`\n\n- `start_instance`: use `crud.get_targets_for_instance()` instead of `crud.get_targets(db, instance_id=...)`\n- `DatabaseNotifier.__init__`: accept `instance_name`, `instance_grid`; set them on event in `send()` before other notifiers run\n- `_broadcast_event`: include `instance_name` + `instance_grid` in WS payload\n- `test_notification`: add instance_name/grid to test event\n\n### 7. `web/backend/main.py` — seed on startup\n\nCall `crud.seed_global_targets()` after `init_db()`, before auto-start.\n\n### 8. DB migration (run inside container before restart)\n\n```sql\nALTER TABLE sdr_instances ADD COLUMN grid_locator VARCHAR(10);\n-- Recreate watch_targets with nullable instance_id (SQLite can't ALTER COLUMN)\nCREATE TABLE watch_targets_new (...instance_id INTEGER NULL...);\nINSERT INTO watch_targets_new SELECT * FROM watch_targets;\nDROP TABLE watch_targets;\nALTER TABLE watch_targets_new RENAME TO watch_targets;\n-- Set known grids\nUPDATE sdr_instances SET grid_locator='JN66UO' WHERE name LIKE '%OE8CNI%';\nUPDATE sdr_instances SET grid_locator='JN66TO' WHERE name LIKE '%OE8YML%';\nUPDATE sdr_instances SET grid_locator='JN66SS' WHERE name LIKE '%OE8CXC%';\n```\n\nEmbed as Python script `migrate_v2_global_targets.py` run via `docker exec`.\n\n### 9. Frontend — types + API client\n\n- `SdrInstance`: add `grid_locator: string | null`\n- `WatchTarget`: change `instance_id: number` → `instance_id: number | null`\n- `targets.create` data: `instance_id: number | null`\n\n### 10. Frontend — InstanceForm.tsx + InstanceList.tsx\n\n- InstanceForm: add grid_locator input field after URL\n- InstanceList: show `[JN66TO]` next to instance URL\n\n### 11. Frontend — TargetForm.tsx + TargetList.tsx\n\n- TargetForm: instance dropdown gets `\"Global (alle Instanzen)\"` option (value `null`)\n- TargetList: show \"Global\" for targets with `instance_id === null`\n\n### 12. Frontend — EventFeed.tsx + Dashboard.tsx\n\n- LiveEvent type: add `instance_name?`, `instance_grid?`\n- EventFeed: show `@ OE8CNI (JN66UO)` per event\n\n### 13. `web/frontend/src/utils/strings.ts` — new strings\n\n```\ncfg_grid_locator: 'Maidenhead-Locator'\ncfg_global_target: 'Global (alle Instanzen)'\n```\n\n## Files\n\n| File | Action |\n|------|--------|\n| `bandwacht.py` | Add fields to DetectionEvent, update 5 notification backends |\n| `web/backend/models.py` | Nullable instance_id, add grid_locator |\n| `web/backend/schemas.py` | Update Instance + Target schemas |\n| `web/backend/crud.py` | New queries + seed function |\n| `web/backend/routers/targets.py` | Allow null instance_id |\n| `web/backend/services/monitor_manager.py` | Global targets loading, event enrichment |\n| `web/backend/main.py` | Seed on startup |\n| `migrate_v2_global_targets.py` | **New** — DB migration script |\n| `web/frontend/src/types/index.ts` | grid_locator, nullable instance_id |\n| `web/frontend/src/api/client.ts` | nullable instance_id in create |\n| `web/frontend/src/utils/strings.ts` | New German strings |\n| `web/frontend/src/components/config/InstanceForm.tsx` | Grid locator field |\n| `web/frontend/src/components/config/InstanceList.tsx` | Show grid |\n| `web/frontend/src/components/config/TargetForm.tsx` | Global option in dropdown |\n| `web/frontend/src/components/config/TargetList.tsx` | Show \"Global\" label |\n| `web/frontend/src/components/dashboard/EventFeed.tsx` | Instance name in events |\n| `web/frontend/src/components/dashboard/Dashboard.tsx` | LiveEvent type update |\n\n## Verification\n\n1. Start container → 17 global targets auto-seeded in DB\n2. All 3 instances auto-start and monitor all global targets\n3. Trigger detection → Telegram message includes `Empfänger: OE8CNI (JN66UO)`\n4. Frontend: config page shows global targets with \"Global\" badge\n5. Frontend: new target form allows selecting \"Global (alle Instanzen)\"\n6. Frontend: live event feed shows instance name per event\n"
}
```

     1→# Plan: Add Login with Fixed Credentials
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→The BandWacht web UI is currently completely open — no authentication. Add a single fixed login (username `oe8yml`, random hard password) to protect the tool. No user DB table needed — credentials checked against bcrypt hash, JWT token issued on login.
     6→
     7→## Password
     8→
     9→`Kx8$mQ2vL#nR7wYp`

    28→```
    29→

    31→
    32→- `verify_password(plain, hashed)` — bcrypt verify
    33→- `create_access_token(subject)` — sign JWT with expiry
    34→- `get_current_user` — FastAPI `Depends()` that validates `Authorization: Bearer` header, raises 401 on failure
    35→
    36→### 4. `web/backend/routers/auth.py` — **new** login endpoint
    37→
    38→- `POST /auth/login` — accepts `OAuth2PasswordRequestForm`, checks username + bcrypt hash, returns `{"access_token": "...", "token_type": "bearer"}`
    39→
    40→### 5. `web/backend/main.py` — wire auth
    41→
    42→- Mount auth router (public)

    44→- Protect WebSocket endpoints: extract token from `?token=` query param
    45→

    47→
    48→- Add `getToken()` / `setToken()` / `clearToken()` using localStorage
    49→- Inject `Authorization: Bearer` in `request()`
    50→- On 401 → clear token, trigger `onAuthError` callback
    51→- Add `auth.login(username, password)` and `auth.logout()`
    52→

    54→

    56→
    57→Centered card with username/password fields, dark SDR theme, error message on failure.
    58→
    59→### 9. `web/frontend/src/App.tsx` — auth gate
    60→
    61→- Track `token` state (init from localStorage)
    62→- No token → `<LoginPage>`, valid token → `<AppLayout>`
    63→- Pass `onLogout` to layout
    64→
    65→### 10. `web/frontend/src/components/layout/Header.tsx` — logout button
    66→
    67→Add LogOut icon button that clears token.
    68→
    69→### 11. `web/frontend/src/utils/strings.ts` — German auth strings
    70→
    71→### 12. WebSocket connections — pass `?token=` query param
    72→
    73→Update spectrum and events WebSocket URLs in Dashboard components.
    74→
    75→## Files
